23/// Shared HELP text for the supervised-loop counters that are
24/// registered TWICE: eagerly at boot (this module — so `rate() > 0`
25/// alerts can see the FIRST fire) and lazily at the observation site
26/// (main.rs / reconciler.rs panic arms). The metrics registry is
27/// first-wins on HELP and warns on divergence in debug builds; these
28/// six pairs had drifted into permanent per-tick warn spam (R285).
29/// One const per metric, referenced from both sites.
30pub mod supervision_help {
31    /// HELP for `pv_reconciler_panics_total` (boot + panic-arm sites).
32    pub const RECONCILER_PANICS: &str = "Reconciler tick body panicked and was caught; the reconciler \
33         continues on the next tick";
34    /// HELP for `pv_bridge_maintenance_panics_total` (boot + panic-arm sites).
35    pub const BRIDGE_MAINTENANCE_PANICS: &str = "Bridge maintenance tick panicked and was caught; the loop \
36         continues on the next interval";
37    /// HELP for `pv_bridge_maintenance_errors_total` (boot + error-arm sites).
38    pub const BRIDGE_MAINTENANCE_ERRORS: &str =
39        "Bridge maintenance tick returned an error; the loop continues \
40         on the next interval";
41    /// HELP for `pv_bridge_maintenance_join_errors_total` (boot + join-error sites).
42    pub const BRIDGE_MAINTENANCE_JOIN_ERRORS: &str =
43        "Bridge maintenance tick's spawned task failed to join (panic \
44         or cancellation); the loop continues on the next interval";
45    /// HELP for `pv_atif_retention_panics_total` (boot + panic-arm sites).
46    pub const ATIF_RETENTION_PANICS: &str = "ATIF retention sweep tick panicked and was caught; the loop \
47         continues on the next hour";
48    /// HELP for `pv_atif_retention_errors_total` (boot + error-arm sites).
49    pub const ATIF_RETENTION_ERRORS: &str =
50        "ATIF retention sweep tick returned an error; the loop continues \
51         on the next hour";
52}

61/// Shared HELP for `pv_shutdown_session_close_timeouts_total`, registered
62/// at boot (pre-registration in the pipeline's metric block) and
63/// looked up again on the shutdown path (`main.rs` finalize loop). The
64/// registry is first-wins on HELP and warns on drift — a single const is
65/// the only arrangement that cannot drift.
66pub const SESSION_CLOSE_TIMEOUTS_HELP: &str =
67    "Per-session close hit the shutdown-time per-session deadline (3 s) and \
68     was deferred to restart-time spool recovery. A sustained rate > 0 on \
69     every rollout indicates a class of sessions that regularly hang their \
70     close (leaked leases, dropped worker permits, unresponsive bridge \
71     publish) — the coincident session id in the shutdown warn log is the \
72     correlation key. Distinct from `pv_http_shutdown_drain_timeouts_total` \
73     which fires on the OUTER phase timeout.";
74
75/// Shared HELP for `pv_shutdown_mcp_drain_timeouts_total` (boot
76/// pre-registration and shutdown-path lookup in `main.rs`). Same
77/// first-wins/no-drift rationale as [`SESSION_CLOSE_TIMEOUTS_HELP`].
78pub const MCP_DRAIN_TIMEOUTS_HELP: &str =
79    "Shutdown MCP-inflight drain hit its 5 s deadline before every detached \
80     mcp_call_inner spawn completed. Downstream effects: some detached \
81     tool bodies were interrupted at close and their sessions may be \
82     quarantined at restart-time recovery. Any occurrence during a \
83     rollout indicates a class of tool calls that regularly outlive HTTP \
84     drain (long-running upstream tool, hung sandbox); check the \
85     coincident tracing warn line.";
86
87/// Shared HELP for `pv_idle_close_timeouts_total` (boot pre-registration
88/// and reconciler idle-sweep lookup). Same first-wins/no-drift rationale
89/// as [`SESSION_CLOSE_TIMEOUTS_HELP`].
90pub const IDLE_CLOSE_TIMEOUTS_HELP: &str =
91    "Idle-close reached the per-session deadline (90 s) and returned to \
92     the next tick — indicates a session with an active lease that never \
93     drops (stuck stream, hung worker) or a bridge publish stalled behind \
94     an unresponsive broker. A steady rate > 0 requires operator \
95     investigation of the coincident session id.";

97/// Fallback identity scope when the tool name cannot be parsed from the request.
98pub(crate) const TOOL_INVOKE_SCOPE: &str = "tool:invoke";
99
100/// Identity scope required to invoke a specific tool by name.
101pub(crate) fn tool_scope(tool: &str) -> String {
102    format!("tool:{tool}")
103}

105/// Shared application state passed through HTTP handlers and background tasks.
106#[derive(Clone)]
107pub struct AppState {
108    /// Versioned harness configuration.
109    pub config: Arc<HarnessConfig>,
110    /// Atomic quota and action-budget state.
111    pub store: Arc<dyn StateStore>,
112    /// MCP schema, policy, and action-budget sandbox.
113    pub sandbox: Arc<Sandbox>,
114    /// Event Bridge backend used by asynchronous workers and by lifecycle
115    /// finalization (close/promote publish through it inline).
116    pub bridge: Arc<dyn EventBus>,
117    /// Live session registry.
118    pub sessions: Arc<SessionRegistry>,
119    /// Bounded non-blocking worker queue.
120    pub worker: WorkerHandle,
121    /// Optional NHI validator. Required in production identity mode.
122    pub identity: Option<Arc<IdentityValidator>>,
123    /// Separate revocation namespace for tokens signed by this gateway.
124    pub exchanged_revocations: Arc<dyn pv_identity::RevocationStore>,
125    pub(crate) revocation_audit_window: Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<(Instant, u32)>>,
126    /// Prometheus-compatible metrics registry.
127    pub metrics: Arc<Registry>,
128    /// Pre-resolved metric handles for the request hot
129    /// path so `observe_stage` doesn't take the registry mutex once
130    /// per stage per request. Same series names as the lazy path;
131    /// pre-registered at boot in `new_with_backends_and_metrics`.
132    pub(crate) hot_metrics: Arc<HotMetrics>,
133    /// Reused upstream HTTP client.
134    pub client: reqwest::Client,
135    /// HTTP client for tool backends (private addresses only when
136    /// `require_private_backends` is on; otherwise the shared client).
137    pub(crate) backend_client: reqwest::Client,
138    /// Static credential injected into every chat-completions forward
139    /// (resolved once at startup; value is marked sensitive).
140    pub(crate) upstream_auth: Option<(HeaderName, HeaderValue)>,
141    /// Bearer credential injected into every tool-upstream forward.
142    pub(crate) tool_auth: Option<HeaderValue>,
143    /// Serializes tool-audit completion per execution key (see
144    /// `routes.rs::complete_tool_audit`): an Unaudited replay racing a
145    /// fresh completion for the same key would otherwise submit a second
146    /// `tool_completed` worker job — a duplicate execution record on the
147    /// audit stream and in the signed chain.
148    pub(crate) tool_audit_gates:
149        Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<std::collections::HashMap<String, Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>>>>>,
150    /// Execution keys whose `tool_completed` audit job has been durably
151    /// journaled in this process but whose `.audited` marker write failed;
152    /// a retry must re-attempt the marker without re-emitting the event.
153    pub(crate) tool_audits_emitted: Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<std::collections::HashSet<String>>>,
154    /// The upstream provider's wire dialect, selected by
155    /// `config.provider` at boot. `forward_chat` translates requests
156    /// and responses for the non-OpenAI dialects so the relay always
157    /// sees OpenAI-shaped bytes.
158    pub(crate) provider: crate::provider::ProviderKind,
159    /// Asynchronous session close and promotion service.
160    pub finalizer: Finalizer,
161    /// Flips to `true` when shutdown starts. `/readyz` reads this and
162    /// reports 503 so a Kubernetes readinessProbe (or any external LB)
163    /// stops routing new traffic to a draining pod before axum's
164    /// graceful drain begins.
165    pub draining: Arc<std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool>,
166    /// Sliding one-minute budget for FULL identity-
167    /// rejection audit records (window start, records written).
168    /// Rejections beyond the budget are counted only — see
169    /// `enqueue_transient_failure`.
170    pub(crate) identity_rejection_window: Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<(Instant, u32)>>,
171    pub(crate) journal_key: [u8; 32],
172    /// Tracks in-flight `mcp_call_inner` detached spawns so
173    /// `finish_shutdown` can await their completion between HTTP drain
174    /// and `worker.wait_idle`. Without this barrier, a client-
175    /// disconnected `mcp_call` running past axum's graceful drain
176    /// could reach `worker.try_submit` AFTER `wait_idle` returned,
177    /// stranding a fresh job across `finalize_sessions` and tripping
178    /// `pv_shutdown_session_close_timeouts_total` on an otherwise-
179    /// recoverable session.
180    pub mcp_inflight: Arc<crate::inflight::InflightTracker>,
181    /// Per-principal token-rate limits pushed from the console
182    /// (`policy_bundle_file`); empty until a bundle with rate policies is
183    /// applied.
184    pub pushed_rates: Arc<crate::policy_push::RateLimits>,
185    /// Admission semaphore bounding concurrent `mcp_call_inner`
186    /// executions to `config.mcp_concurrency`. R70 F1 (landed R71):
187    /// without this limit a slow-tool-upstream attack + N
188    /// concurrent MCP callers each buffering up to
189    /// `MAX_TOOL_RESPONSE_BYTES = 16 MiB` in
190    /// `read_limited_tool_response` stacked N × 16 MiB unbounded.
191    /// `mcp_inflight` above is a shutdown-drain COUNTER — not a
192    /// limiter. Chat path already has admission via
193    /// `worker.try_reserve_pair`; MCP was the odd one out.
194    ///
195    /// Refused admissions increment `pv_mcp_admission_refusals_
196    /// total` (see mcp_call in routes.rs) and respond 503 with a
197    /// Retry-After header — 503 not 429 because the refusal is a
198    /// server-side capacity signal (matches breaker-open 503-not-
199    /// 429 discipline documented in finalize_error_response.rs).
200    pub mcp_admission: Arc<tokio::sync::Semaphore>,
201    /// Pre-resolved `Arc<Counter>` handle for
202    /// `pv_mcp_admission_refusals_total`. R72 review F2 (landed
203    /// R74): the R72 eager registration removed only
204    /// `HashMap::insert` + `String::to_owned` from the refusal-
205    /// arm cost; the `.counter(NAME, HELP)` lookup itself still
206    /// took `metrics.lock()` (a global parking_lot::Mutex across
207    /// the entire registry) on every refusal. Storing the handle
208    /// here — matching the HotMetrics discipline — lets the
209    /// refusal arm call `.inc()` directly with zero registry
210    /// contention.
211    pub(crate) mcp_admission_refusals_counter: Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>,
212    /// Per-backend credential routing (pillar 4). Resolves tool names to
213    /// their designated backend and attaches the pre-resolved credential.
214    pub backend_router: Arc<crate::backend::BackendRouter>,
215    /// Local policy decision point (pillar 2). Maps tool names to business
216    /// intents and enforces mission constraints per call.
217    pub pdp: Arc<crate::authz::PolicyDecisionPoint>,
218    /// Token signer (pillar 3). Signs exchange tokens and intent tokens
219    /// with EdDSA. Present only when `token_exchange_seed_file` is configured.
220    pub token_signer: Option<Arc<crate::authz::TokenSigner>>,
221    /// Redaction engine (pillar 5). Strips sensitive data from event
222    /// payloads and ATIF step text before they are journaled or published.
223    pub redaction: Option<Arc<pv_redact::RedactionEngine>>,
224    /// MCP Streamable HTTP client and per-agent-session backend sessions
225    /// for backends with `transport = "mcp"`.
226    pub mcp_backends: Arc<crate::mcp_client::McpBackendClient>,
227    /// Signs and verifies the `MCP-Session-Id` values this gateway issues.
228    pub(crate) mcp_sessions: Arc<crate::mcp::SessionSigner>,
229    /// External AuthZEN policy decision point, when `[authzen]` is set.
230    pub authzen: Option<Arc<crate::authzen::AuthzenClient>>,
231    /// Content sink slot shared with the audit worker (tenant telemetry).
232    pub(crate) content_sink: crate::content::SharedContentSink,
233    /// Workload assertion verifier, when `[workload_identity]` is set.
234    pub(crate) workload: crate::workload::SharedWorkloadIssuer,
235    /// Semantic judge, when `[judge]` is set. The sandbox carries the
236    /// matching threshold policy.
237    pub judge: Option<Arc<crate::judge::JudgeService>>,
238    /// The judge's hooks, shared with the audit worker.
239    pub(crate) judge_hooks: crate::judge::SharedJudgeHooks,
240}
241
242/// Pre-resolved metric handles for the request hot path so the
243/// per-stage histogram observation doesn't take the registry mutex.
244/// Same series names as the lazy path — the label suffix matches
245/// `pv_stage_duration_seconds{stage="…"}` exactly.
246///
247/// Ordering of the `[Stage; 5]` arrays mirrors the boot registration
248/// in `new_with_backends_and_metrics`: identity, quota, sanitize,
249/// compression, dispatch. `Stage::index()` returns the offset so
250/// `observe_stage` is O(1) with no string alloc on the hot path.
251pub(crate) struct HotMetrics {
252    pub(crate) stage_histograms: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Histogram>; 5],
253    pub(crate) stage_strict_budget_counters: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 5],
254    /// `config.strict_stage_budget || PV_STRICT_BUDGET`
255    /// resolved ONCE at construction. `std::env::var` was previously
256    /// called per stage per request because `||` cannot short-circuit
257    /// a false config default.
258    pub(crate) strict_stage_budget: bool,
259    /// Pre-resolved `pv_requests_total{route,status_class}` counters,
260    /// indexed by `(Route::index(), StatusClass::index())`. Populated
261    /// at boot; every hit is O(1) with zero string alloc and zero
262    /// registry-mutex acquisition — R58 audit found the previous
263    /// per-request `metrics.counter(&format!("..."))` path did 4
264    /// mutex ops on the shared `Registry` per HTTP request plus
265    /// ~120 B of allocator churn (at 10k req/s: ~40k mutex ops/s +
266    /// 1.2 MB/s allocs on the request middleware alone).
267    pub(crate) request_counters: [[Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 5]; 15],
268    /// Pre-resolved `pv_request_duration_seconds{route}` histograms,
269    /// indexed by `Route::index()`. Same rationale as
270    /// `request_counters` above; the histogram fetch used to hit
271    /// TWO Registry mutex ops per request (base_kinds + metrics
272    /// map).
273    pub(crate) request_duration_histograms: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Histogram>; 15],
274    /// Pre-resolved `pv_upstream_latency_seconds` histogram — hit
275    /// on every successful chat forward. R59 continuation of R58's
276    /// hot-path pre-resolution: prior code called
277    /// `metrics.histogram_with_bounds("pv_upstream_latency_seconds",
278    /// ...)` per request, doing 2 Registry mutex ops per chat.
279    pub(crate) upstream_latency_histogram: Arc<pv_core::metrics::Histogram>,
280    /// Pre-resolved `pv_upstream_errors_total{kind}` counters,
281    /// indexed by `UpstreamErrorKind::index()`. Prior code called
282    /// `metrics.counter(&format!("pv_upstream_errors_total{...}"))`
283    /// per error, doing 2 Registry mutex ops + a String alloc per
284    /// upstream failure. Kinds order matches boot pre-registration.
285    pub(crate) upstream_errors_counters: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 4],
286    /// Pre-resolved `pv_dashboard_requests_total{endpoint,status}`
287    /// counters, indexed by `(DashboardEndpoint::index(),
288    /// DashboardStatus::index())`. Same rationale as
289    /// `request_counters`; the dashboard is lower volume but
290    /// hits the same shared Registry mutex.
291    pub(crate) dashboard_request_counters: [[Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 2]; 3],
292    /// Pre-resolved `pv_dashboard_request_duration_seconds{endpoint}`
293    /// histograms, indexed by `DashboardEndpoint::index()`.
294    pub(crate) dashboard_request_duration_histograms: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Histogram>; 3],
295}
296
297#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug)]
298pub(crate) enum Stage {
299    Identity,
300    Quota,
301    Sanitize,
302    Compression,
303    Dispatch,
304}
305
306impl Stage {
307    pub(crate) const ORDER: [Stage; 5] = [
308        Stage::Identity,
309        Stage::Quota,
310        Stage::Sanitize,
311        Stage::Compression,
312        Stage::Dispatch,
313    ];
314
315    pub(crate) fn label(self) -> &'static str {
316        match self {
317            Stage::Identity => "identity",
318            Stage::Quota => "quota",
319            Stage::Sanitize => "sanitize",
320            Stage::Compression => "compression",
321            Stage::Dispatch => "dispatch",
322        }
323    }
324
325    pub(crate) fn index(self) -> usize {
326        match self {
327            Stage::Identity => 0,
328            Stage::Quota => 1,
329            Stage::Sanitize => 2,
330            Stage::Compression => 3,
331            Stage::Dispatch => 4,
332        }
333    }
334
335    fn from_label(label: &str) -> Option<Self> {
336        Stage::ORDER.into_iter().find(|s| s.label() == label)
337    }
338}

340/// HTTP route label enum. Mirrors the closed vocabulary in
341/// `route_label(path)` — pre-resolved into a fixed-index array of
342/// `Arc<Counter>` / `Arc<Histogram>` so the request-metrics
343/// middleware never touches the shared `Registry` mutex on the hot
344/// path (see `HotMetrics::request_counters`).
345#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug)]
346pub(crate) enum Route {
347    Chat,
348    Mcp,
349    Health,
350    Livez,
351    Readyz,
352    Metrics,
353    SessionClose,
354    SessionPromote,
355    Dashboard,
356    TokenExchange,
357    Revoke,
358    Introspect,
359    AdminRevocation,
360    Jwks,
361    Other,
362}
363
364impl Route {
365    pub(crate) const ORDER: [Route; 15] = [
366        Route::Chat,
367        Route::Mcp,
368        Route::Health,
369        Route::Livez,
370        Route::Readyz,
371        Route::Metrics,
372        Route::SessionClose,
373        Route::SessionPromote,
374        Route::Dashboard,
375        Route::TokenExchange,
376        Route::Revoke,
377        Route::Introspect,
378        Route::AdminRevocation,
379        Route::Jwks,
380        Route::Other,
381    ];
382
383    pub(crate) fn label(self) -> &'static str {
384        match self {
385            Route::Chat => "chat",
386            Route::Mcp => "mcp",
387            Route::Health => "health",
388            Route::Livez => "livez",
389            Route::Readyz => "readyz",
390            Route::Metrics => "metrics",
391            Route::SessionClose => "session_close",
392            Route::SessionPromote => "session_promote",
393            Route::Dashboard => "dashboard",
394            Route::TokenExchange => "token_exchange",
395            Route::Revoke => "revoke",
396            Route::Introspect => "introspect",
397            Route::AdminRevocation => "admin_revocation",
398            Route::Jwks => "jwks",
399            Route::Other => "other",
400        }
401    }
402
403    pub(crate) fn index(self) -> usize {
404        match self {
405            Route::Chat => 0,
406            Route::Mcp => 1,
407            Route::Health => 2,
408            Route::Livez => 3,
409            Route::Readyz => 4,
410            Route::Metrics => 5,
411            Route::SessionClose => 6,
412            Route::SessionPromote => 7,
413            Route::Dashboard => 8,
414            Route::TokenExchange => 9,
415            Route::Revoke => 10,
416            Route::Introspect => 11,
417            Route::AdminRevocation => 12,
418            Route::Jwks => 13,
419            Route::Other => 14,
420        }
421    }
422}

424/// HTTP status-class label. Mirrors the closed vocabulary in the
425/// request-metrics middleware. Kept as a separate enum so future
426/// additions (e.g., a hypothetical `2xx-slow`) surface via
427/// exhaustive-match compile checks.
428#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug)]
429pub(crate) enum StatusClass {
430    Informational, // 1xx
431    Success,       // 2xx
432    Redirection,   // 3xx
433    ClientError,   // 4xx
434    ServerError,   // 5xx
435}
436
437impl StatusClass {
438    pub(crate) const ORDER: [StatusClass; 5] = [
439        StatusClass::Informational,
440        StatusClass::Success,
441        StatusClass::Redirection,
442        StatusClass::ClientError,
443        StatusClass::ServerError,
444    ];
445
446    pub(crate) fn label(self) -> &'static str {
447        match self {
448            StatusClass::Informational => "1xx",
449            StatusClass::Success => "2xx",
450            StatusClass::Redirection => "3xx",
451            StatusClass::ClientError => "4xx",
452            StatusClass::ServerError => "5xx",
453        }
454    }
455
456    pub(crate) fn index(self) -> usize {
457        match self {
458            StatusClass::Informational => 0,
459            StatusClass::Success => 1,
460            StatusClass::Redirection => 2,
461            StatusClass::ClientError => 3,
462            StatusClass::ServerError => 4,
463        }
464    }
465
466    pub(crate) fn from_u16(status: u16) -> Self {
467        match status {
468            100..=199 => StatusClass::Informational,
469            200..=299 => StatusClass::Success,
470            300..=399 => StatusClass::Redirection,
471            400..=499 => StatusClass::ClientError,
472            _ => StatusClass::ServerError,
473        }
474    }
475}
476
477/// Upstream error classifier used for `pv_upstream_errors_total{kind}`.
478/// Mirrors `classify_upstream_error(err)` — same set as the boot
479/// pre-registration in `Pipeline::new` at pipeline.rs:511.
480#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug)]
481pub(crate) enum UpstreamErrorKind {
482    Timeout,
483    Connect,
484    Send,
485    Http5xx,
486}
487
488impl UpstreamErrorKind {
489    pub(crate) const ORDER: [UpstreamErrorKind; 4] = [
490        UpstreamErrorKind::Timeout,
491        UpstreamErrorKind::Connect,
492        UpstreamErrorKind::Send,
493        UpstreamErrorKind::Http5xx,
494    ];
495
496    pub(crate) fn label(self) -> &'static str {
497        match self {
498            UpstreamErrorKind::Timeout => "timeout",
499            UpstreamErrorKind::Connect => "connect",
500            UpstreamErrorKind::Send => "send",
501            UpstreamErrorKind::Http5xx => "http_5xx",
502        }
503    }
504
505    pub(crate) fn index(self) -> usize {
506        match self {
507            UpstreamErrorKind::Timeout => 0,
508            UpstreamErrorKind::Connect => 1,
509            UpstreamErrorKind::Send => 2,
510            UpstreamErrorKind::Http5xx => 3,
511        }
512    }
513
514    /// Convert `classify_upstream_error`'s `&'static str` output into
515    /// a typed variant. The classifier at pipeline.rs:2736 already
516    /// exhaustively covers this set; this fn is the typed mirror.
517    pub(crate) fn from_reqwest(error: &reqwest::Error) -> Self {
518        if error.is_timeout() {
519            UpstreamErrorKind::Timeout
520        } else if error.is_connect() {
521            UpstreamErrorKind::Connect
522        } else {
523            UpstreamErrorKind::Send
524        }
525    }
526}

528/// Dashboard endpoint label. Mirrors the `record()` call sites in
529/// dashboard.rs (list, detail, stats).
530#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug)]
531pub(crate) enum DashboardEndpoint {
532    Stats,
533    List,
534    Detail,
535}
536
537impl DashboardEndpoint {
538    pub(crate) const ORDER: [DashboardEndpoint; 3] = [
539        DashboardEndpoint::Stats,
540        DashboardEndpoint::List,
541        DashboardEndpoint::Detail,
542    ];
543
544    pub(crate) fn label(self) -> &'static str {
545        match self {
546            DashboardEndpoint::Stats => "stats",
547            DashboardEndpoint::List => "list",
548            DashboardEndpoint::Detail => "detail",
549        }
550    }
551
552    pub(crate) fn index(self) -> usize {
553        match self {
554            DashboardEndpoint::Stats => 0,
555            DashboardEndpoint::List => 1,
556            DashboardEndpoint::Detail => 2,
557        }
558    }
559}

561/// Dashboard endpoint response status label. Mirrors the `record()`
562/// call sites (`ok`, `not_found`) in dashboard.rs.
563#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug)]
564pub(crate) enum DashboardStatus {
565    Ok,
566    NotFound,
567}
568
569impl DashboardStatus {
570    pub(crate) const ORDER: [DashboardStatus; 2] = [DashboardStatus::Ok, DashboardStatus::NotFound];
571
572    pub(crate) fn label(self) -> &'static str {
573        match self {
574            DashboardStatus::Ok => "ok",
575            DashboardStatus::NotFound => "not_found",
576        }
577    }
578
579    pub(crate) fn index(self) -> usize {
580        match self {
581            DashboardStatus::Ok => 0,
582            DashboardStatus::NotFound => 1,
583        }
584    }
585}
586
587impl HotMetrics {
588    fn new(metrics: &Registry, config_strict_stage_budget: bool) -> Self {
589        // Registrations here mirror the boot loop below. Using the
590        // same `metrics.histogram_with_bounds` / `metrics.counter`
591        // getters means these entries are shared with the lazy path
592        // — a caller that still uses `metrics.counter(name).inc()`
593        // hits the exact same Arc<Counter> we cache here.
594        let stage_histograms: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Histogram>; 5] = Stage::ORDER.map(|stage| {
595            metrics.histogram_with_bounds(
596                &format!("pv_stage_duration_seconds{{stage=\"{}\"}}", stage.label()),
597                "Harness stage latency",
598                pv_core::metrics::WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US,
599            )
600        });
601        let stage_strict_budget_counters: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 5] = Stage::ORDER.map(|stage| {
602            metrics.counter(
603                &format!("pv_strict_budget_breaches_total{{stage=\"{}\"}}", stage.label()),
604                "Middleware stages that exceeded the strict per-stage budget",
605            )
606        });
607        // The post-compression quota debit reports under its own label
608        // (`quota_post_compression`, not in `Stage::ORDER`) through
609        // observe_stage's lazy fallback. Pre-register it here with the
610        // SAME wide bounds as the enum stages: first-registration wins
611        // in the Registry, so without this the lazy path created it
612        // with the default bounds and the one stage doing real backend
613        // work (Redis) had incomparable buckets to its siblings.
614        let _ = metrics.histogram_with_bounds(
615            "pv_stage_duration_seconds{stage=\"quota_post_compression\"}",
616            "Harness stage latency",
617            pv_core::metrics::WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US,
618        );
619        // Pre-resolve the request-metrics middleware's counters +
620        // histograms as a [route][status_class] array so the hot
621        // path is O(1) with zero string alloc and zero Registry
622        // mutex ops. Registration order matches the enum ORDER
623        // constants, matched at read time via `Route::index()` and
624        // `StatusClass::index()`.
625        let request_counters: [[Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 5]; 15] = Route::ORDER.map(|route| {
626            StatusClass::ORDER.map(|status| {
627                metrics.counter(
628                    &format!(
629                        "pv_requests_total{{route=\"{}\",status_class=\"{}\"}}",
630                        route.label(),
631                        status.label()
632                    ),
633                    "HTTP requests by route and status class",
634                )
635            })
636        });
637        let request_duration_histograms: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Histogram>; 15] = Route::ORDER.map(|route| {
638            metrics.histogram_with_bounds(
639                &format!("pv_request_duration_seconds{{route=\"{}\"}}", route.label()),
640                "End-to-end HTTP request latency by route",
641                pv_core::metrics::WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US,
642            )
643        });
644        // Pre-resolve upstream latency + error counters — hit on
645        // every chat forward. Same rationale as request_counters.
646        let upstream_latency_histogram = metrics.histogram_with_bounds(
647            "pv_upstream_latency_seconds",
648            "Time to upstream response headers",
649            pv_core::metrics::WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US,
650        );
651        let upstream_errors_counters: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 4] =
652            UpstreamErrorKind::ORDER.map(|kind| {
653                metrics.counter(
654                    &format!("pv_upstream_errors_total{{kind=\"{}\"}}", kind.label()),
655                    "Upstream failures by kind",
656                )
657            });
658        // Pre-resolve dashboard endpoint counters + histograms. Same
659        // rationale as request_counters; lower volume but hits the
660        // same shared Registry mutex.
661        let dashboard_request_counters: [[Arc<pv_core::metrics::Counter>; 2]; 3] = DashboardEndpoint::ORDER
662            .map(|endpoint| {
663                DashboardStatus::ORDER.map(|status| {
664                    metrics.counter(
665                        &format!(
666                            "pv_dashboard_requests_total{{endpoint=\"{}\",status=\"{}\"}}",
667                            endpoint.label(),
668                            status.label()
669                        ),
670                        "Dashboard endpoint requests, labeled by status",
671                    )
672                })
673            });
674        let dashboard_request_duration_histograms: [Arc<pv_core::metrics::Histogram>; 3] =
675            DashboardEndpoint::ORDER.map(|endpoint| {
676                metrics.histogram(
677                    &format!(
678                        "pv_dashboard_request_duration_seconds{{endpoint=\"{}\"}}",
679                        endpoint.label()
680                    ),
681                    "Dashboard endpoint latency",
682                )
683            });
684        Self {
685            stage_histograms,
686            stage_strict_budget_counters,
687            strict_stage_budget: config_strict_stage_budget || truthy_env("PV_STRICT_BUDGET"),
688            request_counters,
689            request_duration_histograms,
690            upstream_latency_histogram,
691            upstream_errors_counters,
692            dashboard_request_counters,
693            dashboard_request_duration_histograms,
694        }
695    }
696}
697
698/// The header name and scheme the upstream credential is sent with.
699///
700/// `upstream_auth_header` / `upstream_auth_scheme` default to
701/// `authorization` / `Bearer`, which is right for OpenAI and every
702/// OpenAI-compatible server. The Anthropic and Gemini native APIs read
703/// the key from their own headers (`x-api-key`, `x-goog-api-key`)
704/// with no scheme prefix, so when the operator left both settings at
705/// their defaults the provider's native header is used instead. An
706/// explicit non-default setting is always honoured as written.
707pub fn effective_upstream_auth_header(config: &HarnessConfig) -> (String, String) {
708    let at_defaults = config.upstream_auth_header.eq_ignore_ascii_case("authorization")
709        && config.upstream_auth_scheme.eq_ignore_ascii_case("bearer");
710    let native = crate::provider::adapter_for(&config.provider)
711        .and_then(crate::provider::ProviderKind::native_auth_header);
712    match native {
713        Some(header) if at_defaults => (header.to_owned(), String::new()),
714        _ => (
715            config.upstream_auth_header.to_ascii_lowercase(),
716            config.upstream_auth_scheme.clone(),
717        ),
718    }
719}
720
721/// How the harness authenticates to the chat upstream, for startup logs
722/// and `pvctl doctor` — never contains key material.
723pub fn describe_upstream_auth(config: &HarnessConfig) -> String {
724    let (header, _) = effective_upstream_auth_header(config);
725    if config.upstream_authorization_passthrough {
726        "passthrough(client Authorization)".to_owned()
727    } else if let Some(env) = config.upstream_api_key_env.as_deref() {
728        format!("api-key from ${env} in header {header:?}")
729    } else if let Some(file) = config.upstream_api_key_file.as_deref() {
730        format!("api-key from file {file} in header {header:?}")
731    } else {
732        "none".to_owned()
733    }
734}
735
736/// Resolve the configured upstream credential into a ready header pair.
737///
738/// Key material is accepted only from an environment variable or an
739/// owner-only file — never from TOML or argv — and the resulting header
740/// value is marked sensitive so `Debug` output redacts it.
741pub(crate) fn resolve_upstream_auth(
742    config: &HarnessConfig,
743) -> Result<Option<(HeaderName, HeaderValue)>, PipelineError> {
744    let key = match read_secret(
745        config.upstream_api_key_env.as_deref(),
746        config.upstream_api_key_file.as_deref(),
747        "upstream API key",
748    )? {
749        Some(key) => key,
750        None => return Ok(None),
751    };
752    let (header, scheme) = effective_upstream_auth_header(config);
753    let name = HeaderName::try_from(header.as_str()).map_err(|_| {
754        PipelineError::upstream(format!(
755            "upstream_auth_header {header:?} is not a valid header name"
756        ))
757    })?;
758    let rendered = if scheme.is_empty() {
759        key
760    } else {
761        format!("{scheme} {key}")
762    };
763    let mut value = HeaderValue::from_str(&rendered).map_err(|_| {
764        PipelineError::upstream(
765            "upstream API key contains bytes that cannot appear in an HTTP header".to_owned(),
766        )
767    })?;
768    value.set_sensitive(true);
769    Ok(Some((name, value)))
770}
771
772/// Resolve the optional tool-upstream bearer token.
773pub(crate) fn resolve_tool_auth(config: &HarnessConfig) -> Result<Option<HeaderValue>, PipelineError> {
774    let token = match read_secret(
775        config.tool_upstream_bearer_env.as_deref(),
776        config.tool_upstream_bearer_file.as_deref(),
777        "tool upstream bearer token",
778    )? {
779        Some(token) => token,
780        None => return Ok(None),
781    };
782    let mut value = HeaderValue::from_str(&format!("Bearer {token}")).map_err(|_| {
783        PipelineError::upstream(
784            "tool upstream bearer token contains bytes that cannot appear in an HTTP header".to_owned(),
785        )
786    })?;
787    value.set_sensitive(true);
788    Ok(Some(value))
789}
790
791/// Read a secret from an env var or an owner-only file, trimming
792/// surrounding whitespace. A configured-but-missing source is a loud startup error:
793/// silently proxying unauthenticated would produce baffling upstream 401s.
794pub(crate) fn read_secret(
795    env_name: Option<&str>,
796    file_path: Option<&str>,
797    what: &str,
798) -> Result<Option<String>, PipelineError> {
799    read_secret_from(|name| std::env::var(name).ok(), env_name, file_path, what)
800}
801
802/// Testable core of [`read_secret`] with an injected environment.
803fn read_secret_from(
804    get_env: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<String>,
805    env_name: Option<&str>,
806    file_path: Option<&str>,
807    what: &str,
808) -> Result<Option<String>, PipelineError> {
809    if let Some(name) = env_name {
810        let value = get_env(name).ok_or_else(|| {
811            PipelineError::upstream(format!(
812                "{what}: environment variable {name} is not set (export it or update the config)"
813            ))
814        })?;
815        let value = value.trim().to_owned();
816        if value.is_empty() {
817            return Err(PipelineError::upstream(format!(
818                "{what}: environment variable {name} is set but empty"
819            )));
820        }
821        return Ok(Some(value));
822    }
823    if let Some(path) = file_path {
824        require_owner_only_secret(std::path::Path::new(path))
825            .map_err(|error| PipelineError::upstream(format!("{what}: {error}")))?;
826        let value = std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|error| PipelineError::Upstream {
827            context: format!("{what}: read {path}: {error}"),
828            source: Some(Box::new(error)),
829        })?;
830        let value = value.trim().to_owned();
831        if value.is_empty() {
832            return Err(PipelineError::upstream(format!("{what}: file {path} is empty")));
833        }
834        return Ok(Some(value));
835    }
836    Ok(None)
837}
838
839/// Same posture as the signing-seed loader: refuse symlinks (a pre-planted
840/// link would fool the mode check, CWE-59) and group/other-readable modes
841/// on Unix. Windows deployments rely on operator-set ACLs.
842pub(crate) fn require_owner_only_secret(path: &std::path::Path) -> Result<(), String> {
843    #[cfg(unix)]
844    {
845        use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt as _;
846        let metadata = std::fs::symlink_metadata(path)
847            .map_err(|error| format!("stat secret file {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
848        let file_type = metadata.file_type();
849        if file_type.is_symlink() {
850            return Err(format!(
851                "secret file {} is a symbolic link; refusing to follow",
852                path.display()
853            ));
854        }
855        if !file_type.is_file() {
856            return Err(format!("secret file {} is not a regular file", path.display()));
857        }
858        let mode = metadata.mode() & 0o777;
859        if mode & 0o077 != 0 {
860            return Err(format!(
861                "secret file {} has mode 0o{mode:03o}; must be owner-only (chmod 600 {})",
862                path.display(),
863                path.display()
864            ));
865        }
866    }
867    #[cfg(not(unix))]
868    {
869        let _ = path;
870    }
871    Ok(())
872}
873
874/// A request after all local hot-path gates have passed.
875pub struct PreparedRequest {
876    /// Session bound to this request.
877    pub session: Arc<Session>,
878    /// Identity validated for this request.
879    pub identity: AgentIdentity,
880    /// Payload forwarded to the upstream provider after compression.
881    pub payload: Value,
882    /// Total local middleware time before upstream I/O.
883    pub middleware_us: u64,
884    /// Prompt tokens debited from the session and principal budgets at
885    /// admission (`ActionBudget::try_tokens`), held as an RAII guard so
886    /// BOTH a provably-not-forwarded explicit abort and a cancelled
887    /// request future (client disconnect at any pre-dispatch await)
888    /// refund exactly what was debited. Disarmed at upstream dispatch.
889    admission_debit: AdmissionDebit,
890    // Fields drop in declaration order. `capture_guard` MUST drop before
891    // `lease` on a cancelled request future — the lease's Drop notifies
892    // `wait_for_streams`, unblocking `close_session_locked`, which then
893    // checks `wait_for_worker_jobs()`. If the guard submits its terminal
894    // job after the lease releases, the close's `pending_jobs == 0` load
895    // can win the race, finalize proceeds, and the guard's job lands
896    // after the receipt was sealed (chain/receipt divergence for signed
897    // sessions; a resurrected step journal after remove_step_journal for
898    // unsigned). AbortFinalizingStream preserves the same invariant by
899    // convention (its Drop runs before `_lease` drops).
900    capture_guard: ResponseCaptureGuard,
901    lease: SessionLease,
902    /// Client `Authorization` header captured for passthrough mode only.
903    client_authorization: Option<HeaderValue>,
904    /// Provider-semantic client headers relayed upstream verbatim, from
905    /// a strict allowlist only (org/project routing and beta gating —
906    /// dropping them broke org-scoped keys and beta features for
907    /// otherwise drop-in SDK clients). Never auth-bearing.
908    upstream_passthrough_headers: Vec<(axum::http::HeaderName, HeaderValue)>,
909}
910
911/// Provider response paired with its active session lease.
912///
913/// For the OpenAI dialect this is the upstream response as received.
914/// For the Anthropic and Gemini dialects the status, headers and body
915/// have already been translated into the OpenAI shape (a
916/// `chat.completion` object, `chat.completion.chunk` SSE frames, or
917/// the OpenAI error envelope), so the relay never sees a native frame.
918pub struct ForwardedResponse {
919    /// HTTP status to relay.
920    pub status: StatusCode,
921    /// Response headers to relay (after the forwardable-header filter).
922    pub headers: HeaderMap,
923    /// Response body bytes, OpenAI-shaped.
924    pub body: futures::stream::BoxStream<'static, Result<Bytes, std::io::Error>>,
925    /// Prompt tokens debited at admission (post-compression heuristic).
926    /// The response relay reconciles this against the
927    /// provider's reported `usage.prompt_tokens` — correcting the
928    /// session totals and refunding the over-charge.
929    pub(crate) billed_prompt_tokens: u64,
930    // Same ordering invariant as `PreparedRequest`: guard drops before
931    // lease so its terminal job registers `pending_jobs` before the
932    // close barrier sees the streams drained. The reserved response
933    // slot travels inside the guard until routes.rs takes it.
934    pub(crate) capture_guard: ResponseCaptureGuard,
935    pub(crate) lease: SessionLease,
936}
937
938/// The upstream request(s) planned for one prepared chat request.
939struct UpstreamPlan {
940    /// Full upstream URL.
941    url: String,
942    /// Request bodies, sent concurrently: one per choice for the
943    /// Anthropic dialect (which has no `n`), otherwise exactly one.
944    bodies: Vec<Vec<u8>>,
945    /// Extra headers every body is sent with (`anthropic-version`, or
946    /// the allowlisted OpenAI passthrough headers).
947    headers: Vec<(HeaderName, HeaderValue)>,
948    /// The credential header, when any.
949    auth: Option<(HeaderName, HeaderValue)>,
950    /// How to translate the answer back; `None` relays it unchanged.
951    translation: Option<Translation>,
952}
953
954/// A natively translated dialect.
955#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
956enum NativeDialect {
957    Anthropic,
958    Gemini,
959}
960
961/// Everything the response translation needs to know about the
962/// request it answers.
963struct Translation {
964    dialect: NativeDialect,
965    /// The client asked for `stream: true`.
966    stream: bool,
967    /// The client asked for `stream_options.include_usage`.
968    include_usage: bool,
969    /// The OpenAI completion id shared by every chunk/choice.
970    id: String,
971    /// The requested model, used until the upstream names its own.
972    model: String,
973    /// Unix seconds for the `created` field.
974    created: u64,
975}
976
977/// Longest upstream error body read for the OpenAI error envelope.
978const MAX_ERROR_BODY_BYTES: usize = 64 * 1024;
979
980/// Longest buffered native response body (matches the relay's own
981/// capture cap).
982const MAX_NATIVE_BODY_BYTES: usize = 16 * 1024 * 1024;
983
984/// Read a whole response body, refusing anything over `cap` bytes.
985async fn read_bounded(response: reqwest::Response, cap: usize) -> Result<Vec<u8>, std::io::Error> {
986    let mut body = Vec::new();
987    let mut stream = response.bytes_stream();
988    while let Some(chunk) = stream.next().await {
989        let chunk = chunk.map_err(std::io::Error::other)?;
990        if body.len().saturating_add(chunk.len()) > cap {
991            return Err(std::io::Error::other(format!(
992                "upstream response exceeds {cap} bytes"
993            )));
994        }
995        body.extend_from_slice(&chunk);
996    }
997    Ok(body)
998}
999
1000/// RAII owner of the journalled response attempt and (once created) the
1001/// durable in-flight response marker, from request admission until a
1002/// downstream owner takes over (`AbortFinalizingStream`, a terminal
1003/// failure job, or the worker's capture publish).
1004///
1005/// Axum drops the request future wholesale when the client disconnects.
1006/// Every *explicit* failure path already retires the marker and closes
1007/// the journalled attempt — but a cancelled future runs none of them:
1008/// the journal kept a dangling non-terminal attempt (crash recovery then
1009/// quarantines the whole session, see `track_response_attempt`) and the
1010/// marker stranded on disk (once the session evicts, every reconciler
1011/// tick re-quarantines its id forever). Drop submits the same terminal
1012/// failure job the explicit paths use.
1013pub(crate) struct ResponseCaptureGuard {
1014    worker: crate::worker::WorkerHandle,
1015    session: Arc<Session>,
1016    identity: AgentIdentity,
1017    marker: Option<String>,
1018    attempt_id: String,
1019    /// The response-capture slot reserved at admission. Owned by the
1020    /// guard so the cancellation path (Drop) submits its terminal
1021    /// record through the RESERVED slot, exactly like every explicit
1022    /// failure path — a saturated worker queue must not be able to
1023    /// poison an admitted session whose slot was pre-paid
1024    /// (`mark_capture_failed` quarantines the id permanently).
1025    /// Explicit paths `take_permit()` when they assume ownership.
1026    permit: Option<ResponsePermit>,
1027    armed: bool,
1028}
1029
1030impl ResponseCaptureGuard {
1031    fn new(
1032        worker: crate::worker::WorkerHandle,
1033        session: Arc<Session>,
1034        identity: AgentIdentity,
1035        attempt_id: String,
1036        permit: Option<ResponsePermit>,
1037    ) -> Self {
1038        Self {
1039            worker,
1040            session,
1041            identity,
1042            marker: None,
1043            attempt_id,
1044            permit,
1045            armed: true,
1046        }
1047    }
1048
1049    /// Hand the reserved response slot to an explicit owner (the
1050    /// SSE/buffered relay, or a failure path that submits its own
1051    /// terminal record). The guard's Drop falls back to the plain
1052    /// worker queue once the permit is taken.
1053    pub(crate) fn take_permit(&mut self) -> Option<ResponsePermit> {
1054        self.permit.take()
1055    }
1056
1057    /// Record the durable in-flight marker so a later drop retires it.
1058    pub(crate) fn set_marker(&mut self, marker: String) {
1059        self.marker = Some(marker);
1060    }
1061
1062    pub(crate) fn attempt_id(&self) -> &str {
1063        &self.attempt_id
1064    }
1065
1066    /// Take ownership of the marker and attempt id, disarming the guard.
1067    /// The caller now owns terminal-record submission.
1068    pub(crate) fn disarm(mut self) -> (Option<String>, String) {
1069        self.armed = false;
1070        (self.marker.take(), std::mem::take(&mut self.attempt_id))
1071    }
1072
1073    /// Disarm in place, returning any marker: for callers that submit the
1074    /// terminal record themselves while retaining the guard (abandon paths).
1075    fn defuse(&mut self) -> Option<String> {
1076        self.armed = false;
1077        self.marker.take()
1078    }
1079}
1080
1081impl Drop for ResponseCaptureGuard {
1082    fn drop(&mut self) {
1083        if !self.armed {
1084            return;
1085        }
1086        let job = refused_response_failure_job(
1087            Arc::clone(&self.session),
1088            self.identity.clone(),
1089            "request_cancelled_before_response_capture".to_owned(),
1090            self.marker.take(),
1091            std::mem::take(&mut self.attempt_id),
1092        );
1093        // Submit through the reserved response slot when the guard
1094        // still owns it (cancellation before an explicit owner took
1095        // over) so worker saturation cannot fail this submission; fall
1096        // back to the plain queue otherwise. Same fallback as every
1097        // explicit refusal path: if the submission fails, fail the
1098        // session closed rather than dropping the capture silently.
1099        let submitted = match self.permit.take() {
1100            Some(permit) => permit.submit(&self.worker, job),
1101            None => self.worker.try_submit(job),
1102        };
1103        if submitted.is_err() {
1104            self.session.mark_capture_failed();
1105        }
1106    }
1107}
1108
1109/// RAII owner of the admission-time token debit (session AND principal
1110/// ledgers), from the post-compression charge in `prepare_chat` until
1111/// the request is handed to the upstream HTTP client.
1112///
1113/// Every *explicit* pre-dispatch abort refunds via `abandon_prepared` —
1114/// but axum drops the request future wholesale on client disconnect,
1115/// running none of them. Without this guard, a cancellation between
1116/// admission and `send()` (the `spawn_blocking` join in
1117/// `prepare_chat_nonblocking`, or the awaited marker write in
1118/// `forward_chat`) leaked the debit on both ledgers; the principal
1119/// ledger persists across sessions and never heals, so a client that
1120/// disconnected mid-admission drained its principal budget with zero
1121/// LLM work.
1122///
1123/// [`Self::take_for_dispatch`] disarms the guard at the last
1124/// pre-dispatch point: from there the request may reach the provider,
1125/// and the debit conservatively stands (mirroring `forward_chat`'s
1126/// `is_connect`-only refund on send errors).
1127pub(crate) struct AdmissionDebit {
1128    store: Arc<dyn StateStore>,
1129    session_id: String,
1130    budget: pv_state::BudgetSpec,
1131    principal: Option<(String, pv_state::BudgetSpec)>,
1132    tokens: u64,
1133    /// Metrics registry for the Drop-path panic supervisor.
1134    ///
1135    /// If the refund closure panics inside the spawn_blocking pool (a
1136    /// backend allocator failure, a parking_lot mutex panic during
1137    /// unwind, a HashMap grow OOM), tokio's default
1138    /// `UnhandledPanic::Ignore` swallows it silently. The debit then
1139    /// stays permanent on BOTH the session ledger and — the material
1140    /// consequence — the principal ledger, which persists across
1141    /// sessions and never heals. Surface the panic via a bumped
1142    /// counter and a tracing::error so `rate(pv_admission_refund_panics_total)`
1143    /// catches it.
1144    metrics: Arc<Registry>,
1145}
1146
1147impl AdmissionDebit {
1148    /// Disarm and hand the debited amount to the dispatch path; the
1149    /// debit stands from here (reconciled against provider usage, or
1150    /// refunded by the explicit `is_connect` arm).
1151    fn take_for_dispatch(&mut self) -> u64 {
1152        std::mem::take(&mut self.tokens)
1153    }
1154
1155    /// Refund both ledgers now, in the order they were spent. Zeroed so
1156    /// a later drop (or a hypothetical double abandon) cannot
1157    /// double-refund.
1158    fn refund_now(&mut self) {
1159        let tokens = std::mem::take(&mut self.tokens);
1160        if tokens == 0 {
1161            return;
1162        }
1163        ActionBudget::new(self.store.as_ref(), &self.session_id, &self.budget).refund_tokens(tokens);
1164        if let Some((principal_id, spec)) = self.principal.as_ref() {
1165            ActionBudget::for_principal(self.store.as_ref(), principal_id, spec).refund_tokens(tokens);
1166        }
1167    }
1168}
1169
1170impl Drop for AdmissionDebit {
1171    fn drop(&mut self) {
1172        if self.tokens == 0 {
1173            return;
1174        }
1175        // A refund is a synchronous store call (a network round-trip on
1176        // the redis backend). A cancelled request future drops on an
1177        // async worker thread, where that round-trip would stall every
1178        // other stream polled there — move it to the blocking pool when
1179        // a runtime is available (drops on the `spawn_blocking` path of
1180        // `prepare_chat_nonblocking` already run on blocking threads,
1181        // and unit tests may have no runtime at all: refund inline).
1182        let tokens = std::mem::take(&mut self.tokens);
1183        let store = Arc::clone(&self.store);
1184        let session_id = std::mem::take(&mut self.session_id);
1185        let budget = std::mem::take(&mut self.budget);
1186        let principal = self.principal.take();
1187        let metrics = Arc::clone(&self.metrics);
1188        // `catch_unwind` around the refund body so a panic inside
1189        // spawn_blocking (backend allocator OOM, parking_lot mutex
1190        // panic during unwind, a HashMap grow at capacity) doesn't
1191        // get swallowed by tokio's default `UnhandledPanic::Ignore`.
1192        // The `store: Arc<dyn StateStore>` and `metrics: Arc<Registry>`
1193        // captures are already `UnwindSafe` (Arc is UnwindSafe when
1194        // its inner T is `RefUnwindSafe`, and the store trait's
1195        // implementors are RefUnwindSafe by convention here).
1196        // `session_id: String`, `budget: BudgetSpec`, and
1197        // `principal: Option<(String, BudgetSpec)>` are all
1198        // Send + UnwindSafe. Wrap the whole thing in
1199        // `AssertUnwindSafe` to bypass the `RefUnwindSafe` check on
1200        // the trait object rather than plumb the bound through the
1201        // whole StateStore hierarchy — the invariant we care about
1202        // (no shared mutable state leaked from the refund) is
1203        // upheld by construction.
1204        let sess_for_log = session_id.clone();
1205        let refund = move || {
1206            let outcome = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
1207                ActionBudget::new(store.as_ref(), &session_id, &budget).refund_tokens(tokens);
1208                if let Some((principal_id, spec)) = principal.as_ref() {
1209                    ActionBudget::for_principal(store.as_ref(), principal_id, spec).refund_tokens(tokens);
1210                }
1211            }));
1212            if let Err(panic) = outcome {
1213                let msg = panic
1214                    .downcast_ref::<&'static str>()
1215                    .copied()
1216                    .or_else(|| panic.downcast_ref::<String>().map(String::as_str))
1217                    .unwrap_or("panic payload was not a string");
1218                metrics
1219                    .counter(
1220                        "pv_admission_refund_panics_total",
1221                        "Admission-debit refund closure panicked in Drop; the session \
1222                         and principal ledgers retain the debit until operator repair",
1223                    )
1224                    .inc();
1225                tracing::error!(
1226                    session = %sess_for_log,
1227                    tokens,
1228                    panic = %msg,
1229                    "admission-debit refund PANICKED (caught); \
1230                     principal ledger drain persists until operator repair"
1231                );
1232            }
1233        };
1234        match tokio::runtime::Handle::try_current() {
1235            Ok(handle) => {
1236                handle.spawn_blocking(refund);
1237            }
1238            Err(_) => refund(),
1239        }
1240    }
1241}
1242
1243/// Terminal failure job used by response-refusal paths (permit missing,
1244/// unsupported content-encoding, cancelled request future). Carrying
1245/// `response_marker` lets the worker retire the durable in-flight marker,
1246/// and `terminal: true` closes the journalled response attempt so crash
1247/// recovery does not quarantine the session over a request the client
1248/// already saw fail (or abandoned).
1249pub(crate) fn refused_response_failure_job(
1250    session: Arc<Session>,
1251    identity: AgentIdentity,
1252    reason: String,
1253    response_marker: Option<String>,
1254    response_attempt_id: String,
1255) -> WorkerJob {
1256    let atif = (session.workflow == Workflow::Unsigned).then(|| AtifCapture {
1257        source: pv_atif::Source::Agent,
1258        message: Value::String(reason.clone()),
1259        reasoning_content: None,
1260        model_name: None,
1261        tool_calls: None,
1262        observation: None,
1263        llm_call_count: Some(0),
1264    });
1265    WorkerJob {
1266        session,
1267        identity,
1268        class: EventClass::StopReason,
1269        payload: serde_json::json!({"reason": reason, "direction": "upstream_response"}),
1270        text: String::new(),
1271        analyze_loop: false,
1272        status: StatusId::Failure,
1273        stop_reason: Some(StopReason::Other),
1274        native_stop_reason: None,
1275        metrics: EventMetrics::default(),
1276        cost_usd_micros: 0,
1277        prompt_token_correction: 0,
1278        atif,
1279        response_marker,
1280        response_attempt: Some(crate::worker::ResponseAttempt {
1281            id: response_attempt_id,
1282            terminal: true,
1283        }),
1284    }
1285}
1286
1287/// Boxed dynamic error source for harness error variants whose
1288/// underlying cause can be any of several concrete types (`io::Error`,
1289/// `serde_json::Error`, `pv_receipts::ReceiptError`, ...). Kept dynamic
1290/// so one variant covers heterogeneous call sites while still exposing
1291/// the typed error through [`std::error::Error::source`] — callers can
1292/// `source().downcast_ref::<std::io::Error>()` to branch on e.g.
1293/// `io::ErrorKind`, and tracing subscribers receive the full chain.
1294pub type ErrorSource = Box<dyn std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static>;
1295
1296/// Hot-path failures, each carrying an HTTP status mapping.
1297///
1298/// Variants that wrap an underlying failure carry it as a typed
1299/// `#[source]` instead of a pre-rendered `String`; `context` keeps the
1300/// exact text the old `String` payloads rendered, so Display output
1301/// (embedded verbatim in the OpenAI-shaped HTTP error bodies) is
1302/// unchanged. `Unauthorized` and `Abort` stay plain strings: their
1303/// messages are deliberately classified policy verdicts (the raw
1304/// identity error is logged separately and must not travel with the
1305/// client-facing value).
1306#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
1307#[non_exhaustive]
1308pub enum PipelineError {
1309    /// A request header was malformed or inconsistent.
1310    #[error("bad request: {context}")]
1311    BadRequest {
1312        /// Human-readable description.
1313        context: String,
1314        /// Underlying typed error, when one exists.
1315        #[source]
1316        source: Option<ErrorSource>,
1317    },
1318    /// Identity was missing or invalid.
1319    #[error("unauthorized: {0}")]
1320    Unauthorized(String),
1321    /// A stateful quota or loop breaker blocked execution.
1322    #[error("request blocked: {context}")]
1323    Blocked {
1324        /// Human-readable description.
1325        context: String,
1326        /// Underlying typed error, when one exists.
1327        #[source]
1328        source: Option<ErrorSource>,
1329    },
1330    /// The upstream provider request failed.
1331    #[error("upstream request failed: {context}")]
1332    Upstream {
1333        /// Human-readable description.
1334        context: String,
1335        /// Underlying typed error, when one exists.
1336        #[source]
1337        source: Option<ErrorSource>,
1338    },
1339    /// The upstream provider accepted the connection but did not
1340    /// finish responding within `upstream_read_timeout_s`. Distinct
1341    /// from [`Self::Upstream`] so the response layer can map to
1342    /// `504 Gateway Timeout` (RFC 7231 §6.6.5) rather than
1343    /// `502 Bad Gateway`, letting operator dashboards distinguish
1344    /// "provider slow" (504 — check provider health) from
1345    /// "provider unreachable" (502 — check DNS / network / config).
1346    /// SDK retry behaviour is unchanged (both retryable).
1347    #[error("upstream request timed out: {context}")]
1348    UpstreamTimeout {
1349        /// Human-readable description.
1350        context: String,
1351        /// Underlying typed error, when one exists.
1352        #[source]
1353        source: Option<ErrorSource>,
1354    },
1355    /// The breaker requested immediate connection closure.
1356    #[error("connection aborted: {0}")]
1357    Abort(String),
1358    /// Required audit capture infrastructure is unavailable.
1359    #[error("service unavailable: {context}")]
1360    Unavailable {
1361        /// Human-readable description.
1362        context: String,
1363        /// Underlying typed error, when one exists.
1364        #[source]
1365        source: Option<ErrorSource>,
1366    },
1367}
1368
1369impl PipelineError {
1370    /// Semantic bad-request refusal with no underlying typed error.
1371    pub fn bad_request(context: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1372        Self::BadRequest {
1373            context: context.into(),
1374            source: None,
1375        }
1376    }
1377
1378    /// Bad request caused by a typed error. The error text is kept in
1379    /// `context` so Display matches the old stringified payload, while
1380    /// the value itself stays reachable via `Error::source()`.
1381    pub fn bad_request_source<E>(error: E) -> Self
1382    where
1383        E: std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static,
1384    {
1385        Self::BadRequest {
1386            context: error.to_string(),
1387            source: Some(Box::new(error)),
1388        }
1389    }
1390
1391    /// Semantic policy block with no underlying typed error.
1392    pub fn blocked(context: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1393        Self::Blocked {
1394            context: context.into(),
1395            source: None,
1396        }
1397    }
1398
1399    /// Block caused by a typed error (see [`Self::bad_request_source`]).
1400    pub fn blocked_source<E>(error: E) -> Self
1401    where
1402        E: std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static,
1403    {
1404        Self::Blocked {
1405            context: error.to_string(),
1406            source: Some(Box::new(error)),
1407        }
1408    }
1409
1410    /// Semantic upstream failure with no underlying typed error.
1411    pub fn upstream(context: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1412        Self::Upstream {
1413            context: context.into(),
1414            source: None,
1415        }
1416    }
1417
1418    /// Upstream failure caused by a typed error (see
1419    /// [`Self::bad_request_source`]).
1420    pub fn upstream_source<E>(error: E) -> Self
1421    where
1422        E: std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static,
1423    {
1424        Self::Upstream {
1425            context: error.to_string(),
1426            source: Some(Box::new(error)),
1427        }
1428    }
1429
1430    /// Semantic upstream-timeout failure with no underlying typed
1431    /// error. Response layer maps this to `504 Gateway Timeout`
1432    /// (see [`Self::UpstreamTimeout`] docs).
1433    pub fn upstream_timeout(context: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1434        Self::UpstreamTimeout {
1435            context: context.into(),
1436            source: None,
1437        }
1438    }
1439
1440    /// Semantic unavailability with no underlying typed error.
1441    pub fn unavailable(context: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1442        Self::Unavailable {
1443            context: context.into(),
1444            source: None,
1445        }
1446    }
1447
1448    /// Unavailability caused by a typed error (see
1449    /// [`Self::bad_request_source`]).
1450    pub fn unavailable_source<E>(error: E) -> Self
1451    where
1452        E: std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static,
1453    {
1454        Self::Unavailable {
1455            context: error.to_string(),
1456            source: Some(Box::new(error)),
1457        }
1458    }
1459    /// HTTP status code corresponding to this failure.
1460    ///
1461    /// * `Blocked` / `Abort` are permanent policy verdicts for the current
1462    ///   session: 403 / 409 stop mainstream SDK auto-retry loops that
1463    ///   would otherwise interpret 429 as transient rate limiting and
1464    ///   burn budget re-hitting the same breaker.
1465    /// * `Unavailable` is transient: 503 is paired with `Retry-After` at
1466    ///   the response layer so intermediaries and clients back off in
1467    ///   bounded fashion (RFC 7231 §7.1.3).
1468    /// * `Unauthorized` is paired with `WWW-Authenticate` at the
1469    ///   response layer (RFC 7235 §3.1).
1470    pub fn status(&self) -> axum::http::StatusCode {
1471        match self {
1472            Self::BadRequest { .. } => axum::http::StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
1473            Self::Unauthorized(_) => axum::http::StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED,
1474            Self::Blocked { .. } => axum::http::StatusCode::FORBIDDEN,
1475            Self::Upstream { .. } => axum::http::StatusCode::BAD_GATEWAY,
1476            Self::UpstreamTimeout { .. } => axum::http::StatusCode::GATEWAY_TIMEOUT,
1477            Self::Abort(_) => axum::http::StatusCode::CONFLICT,
1478            Self::Unavailable { .. } => axum::http::StatusCode::SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
1479        }
1480    }
1481}
1482
1483impl AppState {
1484    /// The per-session budget in force: the local `[budget]` tightened by
1485    /// any console-pushed budget policy (stricter wins on every
1486    /// dimension, so a pushed bundle can never loosen the host config).
1487    pub fn effective_budget(&self) -> pv_state::BudgetSpec {
1488        self.config.budget.tightened_by(&self.sandbox.pushed().budget)
1489    }
1490
1491    /// Build fully wired application state with a bounded worker and shared
1492    /// lifecycle services.
1493    pub fn new(
1494        config: HarnessConfig,
1495        store: Arc<dyn StateStore>,
1496        sandbox: Arc<Sandbox>,
1497        bridge: Arc<dyn EventBus>,
1498        identity: Option<Arc<IdentityValidator>>,
1499        signer: Arc<dyn Signer>,
1500    ) -> Result<Self, PipelineError> {
1501        Self::new_with_embedder(
1502            config,
1503            store,
1504            sandbox,
1505            bridge,
1506            identity,
1507            signer,
1508            Arc::new(HashEmbedder::default()),
1509        )
1510    }
1511
1512    /// Build application state with an explicit embedding backend.
1513    #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1514    pub fn new_with_embedder(
1515        config: HarnessConfig,
1516        store: Arc<dyn StateStore>,
1517        sandbox: Arc<Sandbox>,
1518        bridge: Arc<dyn EventBus>,
1519        identity: Option<Arc<IdentityValidator>>,
1520        signer: Arc<dyn Signer>,
1521        embedder: Arc<dyn Embedder>,
1522    ) -> Result<Self, PipelineError> {
1523        Self::new_with_backends(
1524            config,
1525            store,
1526            sandbox,
1527            bridge,
1528            identity,
1529            signer,
1530            embedder,
1531            Arc::new(NoopVectorSink),
1532        )
1533    }
1534
1535    /// Build application state with explicit embedding and vector backends.
1536    #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1537    pub fn new_with_backends(
1538        config: HarnessConfig,
1539        store: Arc<dyn StateStore>,
1540        sandbox: Arc<Sandbox>,
1541        bridge: Arc<dyn EventBus>,
1542        identity: Option<Arc<IdentityValidator>>,
1543        signer: Arc<dyn Signer>,
1544        embedder: Arc<dyn Embedder>,
1545        vector_sink: Arc<dyn VectorSink>,
1546    ) -> Result<Self, PipelineError> {
1547        Self::new_with_backends_and_metrics(
1548            config,
1549            store,
1550            sandbox,
1551            bridge,
1552            identity,
1553            signer,
1554            embedder,
1555            vector_sink,
1556            Arc::new(Registry::new()),
1557        )
1558    }
1559
1560    /// Build application state reusing a pre-existing metrics registry.
1561    ///
1562    /// `main.rs` uses this so counters registered outside `AppState`
1563    /// (JWKS refresh errors, HTTP shutdown drain timeouts) live on the
1564    /// same registry that gets scraped at `/metrics` — otherwise their
1565    /// samples would be invisible to Prometheus.
1566    #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1567    pub fn new_with_backends_and_metrics(
1568        config: HarnessConfig,
1569        store: Arc<dyn StateStore>,
1570        sandbox: Arc<Sandbox>,
1571        bridge: Arc<dyn EventBus>,
1572        identity: Option<Arc<IdentityValidator>>,
1573        signer: Arc<dyn Signer>,
1574        embedder: Arc<dyn Embedder>,
1575        vector_sink: Arc<dyn VectorSink>,
1576        metrics: Arc<Registry>,
1577    ) -> Result<Self, PipelineError> {
1578        let config = Arc::new(config);
1579        // Every stage of the `pv_stage_duration_seconds` series MUST
1580        // share the same bucket bounds, otherwise
1581        // `histogram_quantile(0.99, sum by(le) (rate(...[5m])))` in
1582        // Prometheus silently produces nonsense: the `le` values from
1583        // different label combinations do not align and the summation
1584        // is undefined. Use WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US for all stages so
1585        // a future stage that spans real I/O fits without a bounds
1586        // migration — the fast stages just get more granular
1587        // low-end buckets they will never light up, which is fine.
1588        for stage in ["identity", "quota", "sanitize", "compression", "dispatch"] {
1589            metrics.histogram_with_bounds(
1590                &format!("pv_stage_duration_seconds{{stage=\"{stage}\"}}"),
1591                "Harness stage latency",
1592                pv_core::metrics::WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US,
1593            );
1594        }
1595        metrics.counter(
1596            "pv_events_dropped_total{stage=\"worker_queue\"}",
1597            "Worker jobs or response-slot reservations dropped, labeled by admission stage",
1598        );
1599        // The OPERATIONS.md alert table names the response_slot stage
1600        // specifically ("Rate > 0 sustained ⇒ response capture
1601        // backpressure"); a lazily-created series renders that alert
1602        // blind until the first drop actually happens.
1603        metrics.counter(
1604            "pv_events_dropped_total{stage=\"response_slot\"}",
1605            "Worker jobs or response-slot reservations dropped, labeled by admission stage",
1606        );
1607        // Documented as an Escalate alert ("sudden increase"); it must
1608        // exist from boot for absent()/increase() alerts to arm on
1609        // healthy nodes that never had a capture failure.
1610        metrics.counter(
1611            "pv_incomplete_sessions_total",
1612            "Sessions refused due to incomplete capture",
1613        );
1614        metrics.counter(
1615            "pv_worker_panics_total",
1616            "Worker job panics isolated by supervisor",
1617        );
1618        metrics.counter("pv_worker_errors_total", "Worker jobs that failed");
1619        metrics.counter("pv_sessions_finalized_total", "Sessions finalized");
1620        metrics.counter("pv_sessions_promoted_total", "Unsigned sessions promoted");
1621        metrics.counter("pv_reconcile_errors_total", "Reconciliation errors");
1622        // pre-register recovery-skipped counters so
1623        // Prometheus `absent()` alerts do not fire on healthy nodes
1624        // that have never hit a per-session recovery error, and so
1625        // dashboards render flat-zero instead of "No data". Each
1626        // series must exist on `/metrics` from boot; `Registry::counter`
1627        // is otherwise lazy and only inserts on first `.inc()`.
1628        metrics.counter(
1629            "pv_signed_recovery_skipped_total",
1630            "Signed sessions skipped during recovery due to per-session errors",
1631        );
1632        metrics.counter(
1633            "pv_signed_recovery_quarantined_total",
1634            "Signed sessions re-adopted as capture-failed quarantines during recovery",
1635        );
1636        metrics.counter(
1637            "pv_unsigned_recovery_skipped_total",
1638            "Unsigned step-journal consolidations skipped during recovery due to per-session errors",
1639        );
1640        metrics.counter(
1641            "pv_atif_trajectory_recovery_skipped_total",
1642            "ATIF trajectories skipped during recovery due to per-session errors",
1643        );
1644        metrics.counter(
1645            "pv_pending_close_completion_failed_total",
1646            "Pending-close completions that failed to finish their tail",
1647        );
1648        // pre-register data-plane series so dashboards
1649        // render flat-zero from boot instead of "No data", and
1650        // `rate() > 0` alerts can fire the first time the bad thing
1651        // happens.
1652        //
1653        // A boot-time completeness test in the same crate
1654        // (`documented_alert_series_are_preregistered_at_boot`) pins
1655        // this set — new fallible arms added in later work must be
1656        // added here or fail CI. That test caught three counters
1657        // (`pv_ephemeral_close_failures_total`,
1658        // `pv_stream_abort_close_failures_total`,
1659        // `pv_stream_abort_no_runtime_total`) that leaked past this
1660        // block as lazy series — the exact defect this pre-registration
1661        // pass exists to prevent.
1662        for kind in ["timeout", "connect", "send", "http_5xx"] {
1663            metrics.counter(
1664                &format!("pv_upstream_errors_total{{kind=\"{kind}\"}}"),
1665                "Upstream failures by kind",
1666            );
1667        }
1668        metrics.histogram_with_bounds(
1669            "pv_upstream_latency_seconds",
1670            "Time to upstream response headers",
1671            pv_core::metrics::WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US,
1672        );
1673        for route in ["chat", "mcp", "session_close", "session_promote"] {
1674            for status_class in ["2xx", "4xx", "5xx"] {
1675                metrics.counter(
1676                    &format!("pv_requests_total{{route=\"{route}\",status_class=\"{status_class}\"}}"),
1677                    "HTTP requests by route and status class",
1678                );
1679            }
1680            metrics.histogram_with_bounds(
1681                &format!("pv_request_duration_seconds{{route=\"{route}\"}}"),
1682                "End-to-end HTTP request latency by route",
1683                pv_core::metrics::WIDE_LATENCY_BOUNDS_US,
1684            );
1685        }
1686        for reason in [
1687            "too_large",
1688            "read_error",
1689            "invalid_json",
1690            "nonconformant",
1691            "unauthenticated",
1692            "provenance",
1693        ] {
1694            metrics.counter(
1695                &format!("pv_atif_recovery_skipped_total{{reason=\"{reason}\"}}"),
1696                "ATIF spool files skipped during recovery",
1697            );
1698        }
1699        metrics.histogram("pv_receipt_sign_duration_seconds", "Receipt signing latency");
1700        // pre-register the stream-close failure counters. Each one is
1701        // written only from an error arm on a background drop path,
1702        // so left lazy they never appear on `/metrics` until the first
1703        // failure — the exact "rate() > 0 never fires on a lazily-
1704        // created series" defect this block exists to defeat. Adding
1705        // one here is cheaper than an alert that never triggers.
1706        metrics.counter(
1707            "pv_ephemeral_close_failures_total",
1708            "Auto-close of a completed ephemeral one-shot failed; \
1709             the session is left open until the idle sweeper reaps it",
1710        );
1711        metrics.counter(
1712            "pv_stream_abort_close_failures_total",
1713            "Background close after a stream abort failed; \
1714             the session is left open until the idle sweeper reaps it",
1715        );
1716        metrics.counter(
1717            "pv_stream_abort_no_runtime_total",
1718            "Stream abort observed no tokio runtime; capture marked failed for \
1719             reconciler retry",
1720        );
1721        // Panic-supervision counters — same lazy-series defect the
1722        // stream-close counters above defeat, applied to every
1723        // background loop's `catch_unwind` arm. Absent-series alerts
1724        // (`absent(pv_reconciler_panics_total)`) fire a false-positive
1725        // on healthy nodes when the counter is registered only inside
1726        // the panic arm, and `rate() > 0` alerts cannot distinguish
1727        // "never panicked" from "never registered" — so the FIRST
1728        // panic that the guardrail exists to catch slips past the
1729        // alert. Pre-register the five outliers alongside the
1730        // stream-close set. `pv_jwks_refresh_panics_total` is
1731        // already eagerly registered at its call site (main.rs
1732        // beside its interval creation); the five below are the
1733        // panic-arm-only stragglers. HELP text lives in the
1734        // `supervision_help` consts (shared with the observation
1735        // sites in main.rs/reconciler.rs) — the registry is
1736        // first-wins on HELP and warns on divergence, and these six
1737        // pairs drifted apart exactly that way (R285).
1738        metrics.counter("pv_reconciler_panics_total", supervision_help::RECONCILER_PANICS);
1739        // `pv_worker_shard_panics_total` fires from the OUTER
1740        // envelope-routing supervisor in worker.rs — NOT from the
1741        // inner job body (that path uses `pv_worker_panics_total`).
1742        // The distinction is load-bearing during incident triage:
1743        // a routing/dispatcher panic points at the shard task
1744        // structure (backlog, channel, batch-boundary logic), while
1745        // a job-body panic points at the specific request that
1746        // panicked. HELP text and comment kept aligned with the
1747        // write site at worker.rs:864.
1748        metrics.counter(
1749            "pv_worker_shard_panics_total",
1750            "Worker shard driver panicked outside a job; supervised via catch_unwind",
1751        );
1752        metrics.counter(
1753            "pv_bridge_maintenance_panics_total",
1754            supervision_help::BRIDGE_MAINTENANCE_PANICS,
1755        );
1756        metrics.counter(
1757            "pv_bridge_maintenance_errors_total",
1758            supervision_help::BRIDGE_MAINTENANCE_ERRORS,
1759        );
1760        metrics.counter(
1761            "pv_bridge_maintenance_join_errors_total",
1762            supervision_help::BRIDGE_MAINTENANCE_JOIN_ERRORS,
1763        );
1764        // ATIF retention loop: same supervision discipline. A silent
1765        // panic in the hourly sweep would let the spool grow unbounded
1766        // until it fills the disk (retention is the only in-process
1767        // reclaim path). Pre-register both counters so `rate() > 0`
1768        // alerts see the FIRST fire.
1769        metrics.counter(
